`.env.example`:

```example
# Copy to .env (git-ignored) in the folder the server runs from (or a parent of it), or set
# these in your MCP client's "env" block.
#
# Quoting: use SINGLE quotes for Windows paths. In double quotes a backslash starts an escape
# sequence, so the whole file fails to load (the server then logs a warning and ignores it).

# Path to the primary Godot executable. Set it explicitly: official Windows builds are
# portable, version-named files (e.g. Godot_v4.5-stable_win64.exe) stored wherever you put them.
# Windows: 'C:\Program Files\Godot\Godot_v4.5-stable_win64.exe'
# Linux:   "/usr/bin/godot"
# macOS:   "/Applications/Godot.app/Contents/MacOS/Godot"
GODOT_PATH=

# Reserved for planned log capture, currently unused by the server. Windows console wrapper:
# Windows: 'C:\Program Files\Godot\Godot_v4.5-stable_win64_console.exe'
# (The editor end-to-end tests use it to show the editor log when a test fails.)
GODOT_CONSOLE_PATH=

# Port of the editor bridge. Must equal the port the plugin listens on: the project setting
# godot_mcp/bridge_port if present, otherwise the editor's own GODOT_DEBUG_PORT, otherwise 9333.
GODOT_DEBUG_PORT="9333"

# Optional: shared secret for the editor bridge. Leave unset normally: the plugin generates a
# token when the editor starts and the server finds it automatically. Set the SAME value for
# BOTH the editor process and the MCP server only if they cannot share the user's data
# directory (containers, Flatpak, different OS users). Any long random string works.
# GODOT_MCP_TOKEN=

# Feedback channel for the maintainers: off, draft (reports can be written but never sent) or full
# (the AI may submit a report after you say yes). Empty means full.
GODOT_MCP_FEEDBACK=

# Path of the GitHub CLI (gh), used only when you let the AI submit a report. Empty means `gh` on PATH.
GODOT_MCP_GH_PATH=

```

`AGENTS.md`:

```md
# Agent Guidelines & Engineering Standards

This document establishes the architectural principles, development workflow, and operational rules for AI agents and human contributors developing or interacting with the Godot MCP Server.

The contribution workflow — branches per Godot version, carrying changes between them, commit and pull request rules — is in [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md). The verification commands below are the same as its Verification section; change both together.

Work on this repository's issues and pull requests — triage, replies, reviews, merges, the board and release readiness — follows the repo-steward skill, [.agents/skills/repo-steward/SKILL.md](.agents/skills/repo-steward/SKILL.md); its autonomy settings bound what an agent posts, closes or merges without asking.

## Mission & Scope

The primary objective of this project is to build an uncompromising, ultra-fast, cross-platform Model Context Protocol (MCP) server providing AI agents with full, reliable control over the Godot Game Engine (4.x+) across Windows, Linux, and macOS.

Agents must treat the Godot Editor as a live, stateful environment while ensuring complete safety, non-destructive mutations, and immediate visual/diagnostic feedback.

## Architecture Guidelines

### 1. Dual-Mode Operation

- **Live Mode (Primary)**: Connect to the Godot Editor bridge via WebSocket on `GODOT_DEBUG_PORT` (default `9333`). All scene, node, and property mutations must execute in real time. The bridge listens on loopback only and every command carries the shared token (`<user data dir>/godot-mcp/bridge-<port>.token` or `GODOT_MCP_TOKEN`); never add a code path that skips either.
- **Headless Mode (Fallback)**: When the editor GUI is not running, fall back to headless CLI invocation (`godot --headless --script ...`) for batch operations, scene scaffolding, and automated testing. Never fail silently if the editor socket is temporarily unreachable; provide clear diagnostic context and suggest launching the editor or falling back to CLI.

### 2. Safety & Undo/Redo Integration

- Every mutation executed within the Godot Editor (adding nodes, altering properties, removing branches) MUST be wrapped in `EditorUndoRedoManager`.
- This ensures human developers retain full visibility and the ability to undo any action performed by an AI agent using standard shortcuts (`Ctrl+Z`).
- Never overwrite scene files (`.tscn`) or resource files (`.tres`) directly on disk when the editor is actively holding those resources in memory without notifying the editor or triggering a resource re-import.

### 3. Cross-Platform Path Handling & Security

- **Path Traversal**: Strictly validate all file paths against directory traversal (`../`). Paths must resolve within the target project's `res://` directory or designated export folders. Host-side file access goes through `src/paths.rs` (`resolve_in_project`); editor-side paths go through `to_res_path` and `find_scene_node` in `bridge_server.gd`.
- **Argument Injection**: Never pass user-controlled strings to the Godot CLI as bare arguments (a value starting with `-` becomes an engine option) or interpolate them into generated GDScript. Validate identifiers with `is_valid_identifier` and require `res://` for scene arguments.
- **Multi-OS Normalization**: Always handle differences in path separators across Windows (`\`), Linux (`/`), and macOS (`/`). Normalize paths before passing them to the engine or returning them via the protocol.
- **Identifier Sanitization**: Validate node names and class identifiers using strict alphanumeric patterns (`^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$`).
- **Resource Integrity**: Maintain Godot UID tracking (`.uid` files and `uid://` references) whenever modifying scenes or assets to prevent broken dependencies in Godot 4.x.

## Implementation Standards

### Core Server Standards (Rust)

- **Zero-Overhead Async I/O**: Use Tokio event loops for stdio JSON-RPC transport and WebSocket bridge communication.
- **Error Handling**: Never use unhandled panics (`unwrap()` / `expect()`) in production code paths. Use explicit `Result` propagation with informative error types.
- **Clean Stdio Separation**: All internal logging and diagnostics MUST be emitted to `stderr` using `tracing`. The `stdout` stream is strictly reserved for MCP JSON-RPC frames. Every child process spawned by the server (editor, game) gets null stdio.
- **Concurrency**: Tool calls run as independent tasks so a slow editor command never blocks `ping` or other calls; `notifications/cancelled` aborts a running call. Never hold a lock across an editor round-trip.
- **Standardized MCP Responses**: All errors must return an informative message and at least one actionable recovery solution.

### GDScript Standards (Editor Plugin)

- Target the Godot minor version of the branch's release line (`godot-vM.N`; `master` targets the next version) with modern GDScript and strict typing (`var count: int = 0`, `func get_node_by_id(id: String) -> Node:`). `plugin.gd` refuses to start on an older editor and warns on a newer one. The line's identity (`MIN_MAJOR`/`MIN_MINOR`, the version string, `config/features` in the dev `project.godot`, `.github/godot-version`) is checked by `tests/version_sync_test.rs`; the list is in CONTRIBUTING.md.
- Anything missing from the line's first Godot release (M.N.0) must be called through `has_method()` + `call()` (see `is_import_running` on lines before 4.7): an unguarded call to a method the running engine lacks is a script error that sends no reply, and the analyzer only warns about it. Run the e2e tests with the Godot build named in `.github/godot-version`; CI uses the same build.
- Read command parameters untyped and validate them (see `PARAM_TYPES`): assigning a wrongly typed value to a typed variable is a script error that sends no reply and leaves the caller waiting for its timeout.
- Utilize standard signals for asynchronous event handling.
- Keep the plugin lightweight with zero external third-party dependencies outside the standard Godot API.

## Quality Assurance & Verification Protocols

Before submitting or committing changes, agents must execute and pass the complete verification pipeline:

1. **Static Analysis & Linting**:

   ```bash
   cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings
   ```

2. **Code Formatting**:

   ```bash
   cargo fmt --all -- --check
   ```

3. **Automated Testing**:

   ```bash
   cargo test --all-targets
   ```

   Includes `tests/version_sync_test.rs`: the version in `addons/godot_mcp/plugin.cfg` must always equal `Cargo.toml` — bump both together — and every file naming the line's Godot version must agree with the version's `+godot.M.N`. New behaviour needs a test first: protocol and tool behaviour in `tests/stdio_protocol_test.rs` / `tests/tools_test.rs` (helpers in `tests/common/`), bridge client in `tests/bridge_client_test.rs`, keeping the addon out of exports in `tests/addon_export_test.rs`, launched processes staying independent of the server in `tests/detach_test.rs`, and a running game in `tests/runtime_e2e_test.rs`.

4. **GDScript Syntax Check**:

   The repo root has its own minimal `project.godot` (it also makes the addon loadable as a plugin for manual testing). The addon declares no `class_name` on purpose: a global class would be listed in every exported game's class list while its script is left out of the export, so keep addon scripts free of `class_name` and load them by path.

   ```bash
   godot --headless --path . --import   # first run only, or after script changes
   godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/plugin.gd
   godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/bridge_server.gd
   godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/export_plugin.gd
   godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/editor/game_relay.gd
   godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/core/runtime_override.gd
   godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/runtime/mcp_runtime.gd
   godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/runtime/ops/rt_input.gd
   godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/runtime/ops/rt_live.gd
   godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/runtime/ops/rt_output.gd
   godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/runtime/ops/rt_result.gd
   godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/runtime/ops/rt_view.gd
   ```

5. **Editor End-to-End Tests** (whenever `addons/godot_mcp/` changed; needs the Godot build named in `.github/godot-version`, ignored by default):

   ```bash
   GODOT_PATH=/path/to/godot cargo test --test editor_e2e_test -- --ignored
   GODOT_PATH=/path/to/godot cargo test --test addon_export_test -- --ignored
   ```

   Set `GODOT_CONSOLE_PATH` as well on Windows to get editor logs in failure output (the editor is started from the console binary when it is set, and `.env` values fill in variables you did not export, so set both when testing a specific Godot version). A `.env` file with double-quoted Windows paths does not load (see `.env.example`) — export the variables instead.

6. **Running game** (whenever the running-game tools, the debugger client, or the GDScript language-server client changed; same Godot build, ignored by default):

   ```bash
   GODOT_PATH=/path/to/godot cargo test --test runtime_e2e_test -- --ignored
   ```

## Tool Invocation Protocols for AI Agents

When interacting with a Godot project, agents must adhere to the following workflow:

1. **Inspection Before Mutation**:
   - Always query `godot_get_scene_tree` or `godot_get_node_properties` before attempting to modify, add, or delete nodes.
   - Confirm parent node paths exist before instantiating child nodes. Node paths are relative to the scene root.
   - `godot_set_node_properties` is all-or-nothing and returns the applied values; read them back instead of assuming.

2. **Validation After Modification**:
   - Run `godot_validate_script` immediately after creating or altering any `.gd` file. Without `project_path`, `godot_create_script` uses the editor's project, else the project the server was started in; it refuses to overwrite without `overwrite: true`.
   - Catch and resolve syntax errors before attempting to run scenes.

3. **Visual Verification**:
   - Use `godot_capture_viewport` to visually inspect scene changes, layouts, and rendering quality when performing level design or UI assembly.

4. **Runtime Monitoring**:
   - After `godot_run_project`, check `godot_get_run_status` and stop the run with `godot_stop_project` when done.
   - Read logs and errors with `godot_run_output`. Inspect the live tree with `godot_game_tree` before `godot_game_set` or `godot_game_call`.
   - Capture the game with `godot_game_screenshot`. A headless game returns an error and no image; do not treat that error as a picture.
   - `godot_game_call` runs only a method named in `permit`.

5. **Feedback to the Maintainers**:
   - When godot-mcp itself blocks or misleads you (a tool fails for a reason its message does not explain, a needed capability has no tool, a description or schema is wrong), record it with `godot_note_feedback` in English: what you tried, what came back, what you expected, one problem per note. Do not note your own mistakes or problems in the user's project.
   - When the request is completed or abandoned, and notes exist, call `godot_draft_feedback`, show the user the complete report, and offer: copy it, open the link, submit it, or discard it.
   - Never call `godot_submit_feedback` without the user's explicit yes in the conversation.

## Completion Protocol (Mandatory Checklist)

Before concluding any development task, every agent MUST verify the following checklist:

- [ ] **Technical Language**: All code, identifiers, comments, and technical docs are in English.
- [ ] **Verification Pipeline**: `cargo clippy`, `cargo fmt --check`, and `cargo test --all-targets` pass; the GDScript syntax check passes for every addon script.
- [ ] **Editor End-to-End Tests**: If `addons/godot_mcp/` changed, `editor_e2e_test` and `addon_export_test` pass with the Godot build named in `.github/godot-version`, on every branch the change lands on.
- [ ] **Runtime End-to-End Tests**: If the running game, the debugger client, or the GDScript language-server client changed, `runtime_e2e_test` passes with that same Godot build.
- [ ] **Tests First**: New behaviour is covered by a test in the matching `tests/` file.
- [ ] **Version Sync**: The version in `addons/godot_mcp/plugin.cfg` equals the one in `Cargo.toml`.
- [ ] **Undo/Redo**: Every editor mutation is wrapped in `EditorUndoRedoManager`, and no `.tscn` / `.tres` held by the editor is overwritten on disk behind its back.
- [ ] **Path and Input Safety**: File paths go through `resolve_in_project` / `to_res_path`, identifiers are validated, and no user-controlled string reaches the Godot CLI as a bare argument or is interpolated into generated GDScript.
- [ ] **Bridge Security**: The bridge still listens on loopback only, and no code path skips the token check.
- [ ] **Rust Hygiene**: No `unwrap()` / `expect()` in production paths, diagnostics go to `stderr` only, and no lock is held across an editor round-trip.
- [ ] **Error Responses**: Every error carries an informative message and at least one actionable recovery step.
- [ ] **GDScript Hygiene**: Strict typing is used, parameters are read untyped and validated, methods missing from the line's M.N.0 are called through `has_method()` + `call()`, and the addon declares no `class_name`.
- [ ] **Policy Documents**: `SECURITY.md` and `CONTRIBUTING.md` are still true after this change (supported lines, reporting channel, checks, branch model).
- [ ] **Agent Workflow**: Scenes were inspected before mutation, `.gd` files were validated after changes, and any run started with `godot_run_project` was stopped.

```

`CHANGELOG.md`:

```md
# Changelog

All notable changes to godot-mcp. The server and the Godot addon always ship with the same
version number. The section for a version is used as the text of its GitHub Release, so every
release needs one (the release workflow fails without it).

Each Godot version has its own release line and branch (`godot-v4.5`, `godot-v4.6`, `godot-v4.7`, …). From 0.5.1 on, one release holds the files of every supported Godot version, and its notes are the section of this file; they mark changes that concern some Godot versions only.

## [Unreleased]

## [0.5.1] - 2026-10-20

First release made for each Godot version separately. It holds the files for Godot 4.5, 4.6 and 4.7 in one release; download the files whose names end in `-godotM.N` for your Godot version. There is no release 0.5.0: a first attempt that published one release per Godot version was withdrawn in favour of this one. The server and the addon changed together: update both.

### Changed (breaking)

- **One build per Godot version.** Files end in `-godotM.N` (for example `godot-mcp-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc-godot4.6.zip` and `godot-mcp-addon-godot4.6.zip`). Pick the files made for your Godot version; see the table in the README.
- The addon refuses to start in a Godot older than the version it was made for and prints which files to install. In a newer Godot it starts and prints a warning naming the files for that version.
- A `GODOT_PATH` that points to a missing file is no longer replaced by another Godot the server finds on its own, which could run a different engine version than the one you chose. The server logs a warning at start, and tools that run Godot fail with a message naming the path. Leaving `GODOT_PATH` unset (or empty) still looks for an installed Godot.

### Added

- `SECURITY.md` explains which versions get security fixes and how to report a vulnerability privately, and `CONTRIBUTING.md` explains how to contribute, including which branch a change belongs to.

## [0.4.0] - 2026-10-16

### Added

- A feedback channel from the AI to the maintainers. `godot_note_feedback` records something missing,
  broken or misleading while the AI works, `godot_draft_feedback` turns the notes into one report
  (versions, platform, related failures; paths, tokens and mentions removed) and shows it to you,
  and `godot_submit_feedback` publishes it as a GitHub issue through your own `gh` after you agree.
  You can also just copy the text or open the prefilled link. Nothing leaves your machine without
  your approval. `GODOT_MCP_FEEDBACK` (`off`, `draft`, `full`) and `GODOT_MCP_GH_PATH` control it.
- The addon leaves itself out of exported games: a new `export_plugin.gd`, registered by the
  plugin, skips every file in the addon folder. It only acts while the plugin is enabled; with it
  disabled, exclude `addons/godot_mcp/*` in the export preset.
- `project_path` is optional when the server runs inside the project. Without it, tools use the
  project open in the editor, else the first folder with a `project.godot` from the server's
  working directory upwards, else the only subfolder that has one. This applies to
  `godot_launch_editor` (where `project_path` is no longer required), the CLI fallback of
  `godot_run_project`, `godot_create_script` and `res://` paths in `godot_validate_script`.

### Changed

- `bridge_server.gd` no longer declares `class_name GodotMcpBridgeServer` (nothing used it; the
  plugin loads the script by path). A global class would stay in every exported game's class list
  even though the addon's files are left out of the export.

### Fixed

- A launched editor or game no longer keeps the server's stdin, stdout and stderr pipes open on
  Windows. An MCP client that waited for the server's stderr to close after stopping it used to
  wait until the editor was closed too.
- Ctrl+C in the MCP client's terminal no longer closes an editor or game started by
  `godot_launch_editor` or `godot_run_project`: they now start detached from the client's console
  (Windows) and in a process group of their own (all platforms).

### Removed

- The unused `GODOT_MCP_URL` setting (`--mcp-url`). A leftover `GODOT_MCP_URL` in the environment
  or a `.env` file is ignored, but passing `--mcp-url` now stops the server with an error.

## [0.3.0] - 2026-10-14

Hardening release. The server and the addon changed together: update both (the addon archive is
part of every release), and note the behaviour changes below.

### Changed (breaking)

- **Godot 4.5 or newer is required.** The plugin refuses to start on older editors and says why
  (`EditorInterface.get_open_scene_roots()` does not exist before 4.5).
- The editor bridge listens on `127.0.0.1` only and every command needs a token. The plugin writes
  it to `<user data dir>/godot-mcp/bridge-<port>.token` and the server reads it from there, so
  both must run as the same OS user; otherwise set the same `GODOT_MCP_TOKEN` for both.
- `godot_create_script` writes only `.gd` files inside a project, refuses to overwrite an existing
  file unless `overwrite: true`, and accepts only plain identifiers for `class_name` and
  `extends_class`. `res://` paths work, with `project_path` or a connected editor.
- `godot_get_node_properties` returns each property as `{type, value}` with the value in Godot's
  text format (for example `Vector2(5, 7)`).
- `godot_set_node_properties` converts values to the property's type (arrays or objects for
  vectors, `#rrggbb` for colors, `res://` paths for resources). It rejects the whole call when a
  property is unknown or a value cannot be converted, and reports the applied values. Before, it
  answered `updated: true` while changing nothing.
- Node paths in responses are relative to the scene root (`Player/Body`, `.` for the root).
- Node paths are resolved inside the edited scene only; the scene root cannot be removed.
- `godot_run_project` accepts only `res://` scene paths.
- `godot_get_project_info` no longer takes the `project_path` argument (it was ignored) and also
  reports `project_dir`.
- Unknown tools are JSON-RPC errors (`-32602`); malformed messages get `-32600` with their id.

### Added

- `godot_capture_viewport` renders the 2D or 3D editor viewport (`view`, `max_dimension`) and
  reports the image size.
- `godot_create_script`: `project_path` and `overwrite` arguments.
- Tool calls run concurrently, so `ping` and other calls are never held up by a slow editor
  command; `notifications/cancelled` aborts a running call.
- MCP protocol version negotiation (`2025-06-18`, `2025-03-26`, `2024-11-05`).
- One persistent, multiplexed connection to the editor with automatic reconnect; an unreachable
  editor is reported in about 0.3 s.
- Project setting `godot_mcp/verbose` to log client connections in the editor, and
  `GODOT_MCP_TOKEN` to share the bridge token explicitly.
- A `.env` file that cannot be parsed is reported with a warning instead of being ignored
  silently; empty settings (`GODOT_PATH=`) now count as unset instead of stopping the server.
- Continuous integration (Rust on Linux, Windows and macOS, plus a headless Godot 4.5 job),
  53 automated tests and 3 end-to-end tests against a real editor. Releases are gated on
  formatting, lints and tests.

### Fixed

- `godot_run_project` without an editor wrote the game's output into the server's stdout and
  corrupted the MCP stream.
- Scene trees, property dumps and screenshots larger than 64 KiB never arrived (the caller only
  saw a timeout).
- Wrongly typed arguments hung the call for 15 s; they are now rejected immediately.
- `godot_set_node_properties` silently ignored vectors, colors and unknown properties.
- `godot_remove_node` undo restores the node's position and ownership of its subtree.
- `godot_add_node` rejects non-`Node` and abstract classes cleanly and supports global script
  classes.
- `godot_capture_viewport` returned a 2x2 image as a success.
- `godot_resave_resources` pushed scripts and scenes through the importer; it now re-imports
  assets and only refreshes other files. It also no longer hangs on Godot 4.5.
- `godot_run_project` hid editor errors behind a second, detached game process.
- `godot_validate_script` reports a missing file as such, strips the engine banner and times out.
- `godot_create_script` could leave an empty file on Linux and macOS.
- Recovery hints now match the error (connectivity hints are no longer attached to bad paths).

### Security

- The bridge was reachable from the local network and from web pages in a browser, with no
  authentication.
- `godot_create_script` could write to any path, overwrite files without warning, and inject code
  through `class_name` and `extends_class`.
- A scene argument starting with `-` reached the Godot command line as an engine option.
- Node paths such as `..` could read and change the editor's own nodes.

### Notes

- Most of this release is in commit `24fe18a`, whose message lists only the tests.

## [0.2.0] - 2026-09-16

### Added

- Run, stop and query the project through the live editor's Play mode.
- Inspect any open scene tab, and list the open scenes.
- Re-import resources or rescan the project from the editor.

## [0.1.0] - 2026-09-16

### Added

- First release: Rust MCP server over stdio, the Godot Editor bridge plugin (scene tree, node and
  property operations, viewport capture), headless CLI fallback for version, script validation
  and launching the editor.

[Unreleased]: https://github.com/zeegzoog/godot-mcp/compare/v0.4.0...HEAD
[0.4.0]: https://github.com/zeegzoog/godot-mcp/compare/v0.3.0...v0.4.0
[0.3.0]: https://github.com/zeegzoog/godot-mcp/compare/v0.2.0...v0.3.0
[0.2.0]: https://github.com/zeegzoog/godot-mcp/compare/v0.1.0...v0.2.0
[0.1.0]: https://github.com/zeegzoog/godot-mcp/releases/tag/v0.1.0

`CONTRIBUTING.md`:

```md
# Contributing to godot-mcp

Thank you for helping. This guide takes you from a first question to a merged change. It is written for people and for AI agents alike: every rule is a single instruction, and every command can be copied as it is.

| You want to | Go to |
| --- | --- |
| Ask a question or report a bug | [Issues](https://github.com/zeegzoog/godot-mcp/issues) |
| Report a security vulnerability | [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md) (privately, never in an issue) |
| Work on the code as an AI agent | [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md), then this guide |
| Know the license of your contribution | [License](#license) |

## Ways to Contribute

- **Questions:** open an issue. The repository has no Discussions.
- **Bug reports:** open an issue with:
  - the godot-mcp version (`version=` in `addons/godot_mcp/plugin.cfg`), the Godot version and the operating system;
  - the MCP client you use (Claude Code, Cursor, VS Code, …);
  - the steps to reproduce, what you expected and what happened;
  - the server log if it helps: run the server with `RUST_LOG=godot_mcp=debug`; the log goes to stderr.
- **Feature proposals:** open an issue before writing a larger change, so the approach can be agreed first.
- **Security vulnerabilities:** follow [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md). Never open a public issue for them.
- **Reports from your AI assistant:** paste a report drafted by `godot_draft_feedback` into a new issue with the "Feedback from an AI assistant" template, or let the assistant submit it with your approval.

## Branches and Godot Versions

Each Godot minor version has its own release line on its own branch. `master` is the development line for the next Godot version, which has no stable release yet.

| Godot | Branch | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 4.7.x | `godot-v4.7` | Release line, newest stable |
| 4.6.x | `godot-v4.6` | Release line |
| 4.5.x | `godot-v4.5` | Release line |
| 4.8 (pre-release) | `master` | Development line, no releases |

Choose the branch for your pull request:

| The change … | Open the pull request against |
| --- | --- |
| fixes a problem that exists on `master` | `master`; maintainers carry it to the older lines it affects |
| fixes a problem that exists only on some release lines | the newest of those `godot-v*` branches |
| exists only because of one Godot version | that version's `godot-v*` branch only |
| adds a feature or an improvement | `master` |
| changes shared documentation or CI | `master` |

- Find out which lines a bug affects by running its test on each branch, not by reading the code.
- Files that differ between lines on purpose: `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` (version), `addons/godot_mcp/plugin.cfg` (version), `addons/godot_mcp/plugin.gd` (`MIN_MAJOR`, `MIN_MINOR`), `project.godot` (`config/features`) and `.github/godot-version` (the Godot build the line is tested against). Keep every other file identical on all lines unless a Godot version forces a difference.

## Development Setup

1. Install Rust 1.85 or newer: `rustup update stable`.
2. Install the Godot build named in `.github/godot-version` of the branch you work on, from <https://github.com/godotengine/godot-builds/releases>. On Windows, also keep the `_console.exe` file that comes with it.
3. Clone and build:

   ```bash
   git clone https://github.com/zeegzoog/godot-mcp.git
   cd godot-mcp
   cargo build
   ```

4. Tell the tests where Godot is: export `GODOT_PATH` (and on Windows `GODOT_CONSOLE_PATH`, so a failing editor test shows the editor log), or copy `.env.example` to `.env` and fill it in.
   - Exported variables win over `.env`; export both when you test a specific Godot version.
   - In `.env`, put Windows paths in single quotes. A double-quoted Windows path stops the whole file from loading.

## Making a Change

1. Write the test first. It must fail before your change and pass after it.
2. Put the test where its behaviour belongs:

   | Behaviour | Test file |
   | --- | --- |
   | JSON-RPC framing, session, configuration | `tests/stdio_protocol_test.rs` |
   | A tool's host-side logic | `tests/tools_test.rs` |
   | Agent feedback channel | `tests/feedback_test.rs` |
   | Bridge client | `tests/bridge_client_test.rs` |
   | Editor command behaviour | `tests/editor_e2e_test.rs` |
   | Addon kept out of exported games | `tests/addon_export_test.rs` |
   | Launched processes staying independent of the server | `tests/detach_test.rs` |
   | Running game, input, screenshots, breakpoints, GDScript diagnostics | `tests/runtime_e2e_test.rs` |
   | Version string and release line identity | `tests/version_sync_test.rs` |

   Unit tests live next to the code in `src/`.
3. Follow the engineering standards in [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md): no `unwrap()` or `expect()` in production code, logs to stderr only, every editor mutation undoable, every path checked against the project, strict typing in GDScript.
4. Prove every security or data-safety guard with a test that fails when the guard is removed.

## Verification

Run all of these before you commit. CI runs them again on Windows, Linux and macOS.

```bash
cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings
cargo fmt --all -- --check
cargo test --all-targets
```

When you changed anything in `addons/godot_mcp/`, also check the GDScript and run the editor tests with the Godot build of the branch (`.github/godot-version`):

```bash
godot --headless --path . --import   # first run only, or after script changes
godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/plugin.gd
godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/bridge_server.gd
godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/export_plugin.gd
godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/editor/game_relay.gd
godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/core/runtime_override.gd
godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/runtime/mcp_runtime.gd
godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/runtime/ops/rt_input.gd
godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/runtime/ops/rt_live.gd
godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/runtime/ops/rt_output.gd
godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/runtime/ops/rt_result.gd
godot --headless --check-only --path . --script addons/godot_mcp/runtime/ops/rt_view.gd
GODOT_PATH=/path/to/godot cargo test --test editor_e2e_test -- --ignored
GODOT_PATH=/path/to/godot cargo test --test addon_export_test -- --ignored
```

When the running-game tools, the debugger client, or the GDScript language-server client changed, also run that with the same Godot build:

```bash
GODOT_PATH=/path/to/godot cargo test --test runtime_e2e_test -- --ignored
```

## Commits and Pull Requests

- Write commit messages as Conventional Commits with a scope: `fix(bridge): reject a token that is not a string`. Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `docs`, `test`, `refactor`, `ci`, `build`, `chore`.
- Explain in the commit body why the change is needed when the summary does not say it.
- Keep one logical change per pull request.
- State in the pull request description:
  - what changed and why;
  - which Godot lines the change affects, and how you checked (the test, run on each branch);
  - which checks from [Verification](#verification) you ran.
- Never commit `.env`, an MCP client config with real paths (`.mcp.json`), built binaries or the `.godot/` folder. Commit examples with placeholders instead, such as `.env.example`.
- Leave version numbers and `CHANGELOG.md` sections alone: maintainers set them in release commits.
- Write code, comments, commit messages and documentation in English.

## Issues and Pull Requests

An AI assistant, the repo steward, works the issue tracker and the pull requests on behalf of the maintainers. Every message it writes says that an AI wrote it.

- **What it does:** it gives each new issue a type and a priority, asks for missing details, answers questions, closes duplicates, reviews pull requests, fixes accepted issues through pull requests of its own, and keeps the project board "godot-mcp issues and pull requests" current.
- **What the maintainers decide:** whether the project takes on a feature, whether a contributor's pull request is merged or declined, and every release.
- **Priorities:**

  | Priority | Meaning |
  | --- | --- |
  | P0 | security exposure, data loss, the product unusable, a broken release, failing CI on a branch |
  | P1 | a core function broken without a workaround, or a regression |
  | P2 | broken with a workaround, misleading behaviour or documentation, a wanted feature |
  | P3 | minor or cosmetic, nice to have, questions |

- **Labels you may see:**
  - `status: needs-info` — details are missing. A reminder follows after 14 days, and the issue is closed 7 days after the reminder unless the details arrive. Reopen it, or comment, when you have them.
  - `status: needs-maintainer` — waiting for a maintainer's decision.
  - `status: blocked` — waiting for something outside the project.
- **Reach a person:** ask for a maintainer in a comment, and the assistant hands the issue or pull request over.
- **Security problems:** never in an issue or pull request; follow [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md).

## Carrying Changes Between Lines

A change that affects several lines lands on the newest of them first and is then carried to each older one, newest to oldest. Maintainers usually do the carrying; you may do it yourself:

```bash
git switch godot-v4.7
git cherry-pick -x <commit>    # -x records "(cherry picked from commit <commit>)"
```

- Open the carried copy as its own pull request titled `[4.7] <original title>`.
- Resolve a conflict on that line, and make the test pass with that line's Godot build.
- Skip a line on which the test does not reproduce the problem.

## Planning and Specifications

- The `.design/` folder holds the project's specifications and plan. It is committed and shared, and it is maintained on `master` only; a release branch keeps the copy from the day it was created.
- Maintainers plan and design through the Magic Spec workflow (`/magic.spec`, `/magic.task`, `/magic.run`, `/magic.analyze`), run by their AI agent. Its engine (`.magic/` and the `magic.*` workflow files) is installed locally from its release archive and is git-ignored.
- You do not need the engine to contribute. If your change alters documented behaviour, say so in the pull request, and a maintainer updates the specifications.
- Commit changes to `.design/` separately from product changes, so that product commits carry cleanly to the release lines.
- Never mention `.design/`, specification file names or task IDs in product files: code, tests, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `SECURITY.md`.

## For AI Agents

- Read [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) first and follow it. It holds the engineering standards, the agent workflow and the completion checklist; the commands in [Verification](#verification) are the same as there.
- Pick the target branch with the table in [Branches and Godot Versions](#branches-and-godot-versions).
- Never push, tag or publish a release without the explicit approval of your user — except the actions the repo-steward skill's autonomy settings allow (its defaults, as adjusted in `.github/steward.yml`). Tagging and publishing a release are never among them.
- Work issues and pull requests with the repo-steward skill: [.agents/skills/repo-steward/SKILL.md](.agents/skills/repo-steward/SKILL.md).

## Releases

Maintainers publish releases with the agent skill in `.agents/skills/release/SKILL.md`. In short:

- One release holds the files of every supported Godot line, named `vX.Y.Z` (for example `v0.5.1`). Every release takes the next version number; all lines take it together, and a line without a change of its own only advances its version.
- Each line's version names the line: `0.5.1+godot.4.6` on branch `godot-v4.6`, with the line tag `v0.5.1+godot.4.6` marking its sources. The release notes are one `CHANGELOG.md` on `master`.
- A person approves every tag before it is pushed. The release workflow then builds the files of every line and publishes the release.

## License

godot-mcp is licensed under the [MIT License](LICENSE). By contributing, you agree that your contribution is licensed under the same terms. There is no separate contributor agreement.

```

`README.md`:

```md
# Godot MCP Server

A fast, cross-platform [Model Context Protocol](https://modelcontextprotocol.io) (MCP) server, written in Rust, that lets AI assistants inspect and edit a live Godot 4 editor.

- **One native binary** for Windows, Linux, and macOS (Intel and Apple Silicon). No Node.js or Python.
- **Live editor bridge.** A small GDScript plugin exposes the open scenes over a local WebSocket (loopback only, token-protected). Node edits go through `EditorUndoRedoManager`, so anything the AI changes can be undone with `Ctrl+Z`.
- **Headless fallback.** Without a running editor, the server calls the Godot binary directly to report the engine version, validate scripts, create scripts, and launch the editor or the project.

## Installation

Requirements: **Godot 4.5 or newer**, the godot-mcp files made for your Godot version, and an MCP client such as Claude Code, Claude Desktop, Cursor, VS Code, or Antigravity. No Rust toolchain needed.

### Pick the files for your Godot version

Each godot-mcp release holds the files for every supported Godot minor version. Download the files whose names end in `-godotM.N`, where `M.N` is your Godot version (for example `-godot4.6`).

| Your Godot | Branch | Files to download |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 4.7.x | `godot-v4.7` | the files ending in `-godot4.7` |
| 4.6.x | `godot-v4.6` | the files ending in `-godot4.6` |
| 4.5.x | `godot-v4.5` | the files ending in `-godot4.5` |
| 4.8 (pre-release) | `master` | none; build from source |

Release 0.4.0 and older are one build for Godot 4.5 and newer, with file names that have no `-godotM.N` suffix.

### Option A: let your AI assistant install it

Open your Godot project in the assistant and paste:

```text
Install the Godot MCP server (https://github.com/zeegzoog/godot-mcp) for this Godot project. Use the GitHub release, not a local clone of that repository.

1. Detect the OS and CPU architecture (Windows, Linux, or macOS; x86_64 or arm64).
2. Ask me for the full path to my Godot executable. Never guess it: Windows builds are portable, version-named files (e.g. Godot_v4.5-stable_win64.exe) stored wherever I put them. Run `<path> --version` to confirm it works, and note its major.minor version (4.6.3.stable.official → 4.6).
3. Take the files for that Godot version from the latest release: `gh release view --repo zeegzoog/godot-mcp --json tagName,assets` lists them; use the assets whose names end in `-godot<major.minor>` (with a tag like `v0.5.1`). If the latest release has no such files, tell me and do not take the files of another Godot version. Download the server archive for this platform and the addon archive, e.g.:
   gh release download <tag> --repo zeegzoog/godot-mcp --pattern "godot-mcp-<target-triple>-godot<major.minor>*"
   gh release download <tag> --repo zeegzoog/godot-mcp --pattern "godot-mcp-addon-godot<major.minor>*"
   Extract the server to a permanent folder outside the project: %LOCALAPPDATA%\godot-mcp\ (Windows), ~/.local/share/godot-mcp/ (Linux), ~/Library/Application Support/godot-mcp/ (macOS).
4. Copy addons/godot_mcp from the addon archive into <project>/addons/godot_mcp and enable it in project.godot:
   [editor_plugins]
   enabled=PackedStringArray("res://addons/godot_mcp/plugin.cfg")
5. Register the server as "godot" in my MCP client config, with the binary from step 3 as the command and GODOT_PATH from step 2 in env. If the config is a project-scoped .mcp.json in a shared repository, do not commit real paths: write a .mcp.json.example with placeholders and add .mcp.json to .gitignore.
6. Ask me to restart the MCP client and (re)open the project in the Godot editor, then call godot_get_version and report the result.
```

### Option B: install manually

**1. Download** the server for your platform and the addon from the [latest release](https://github.com/zeegzoog/godot-mcp/releases/latest), the files for your Godot version (see [the table above](#pick-the-files-for-your-godot-version)). `M.N` is your Godot version, e.g. `4.6`.

| Platform | Asset |
| --- | --- |
| Windows x86_64 | `godot-mcp-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc-godotM.N.zip` |
| Linux x86_64 | `godot-mcp-x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu-godotM.N.tar.gz` |
| macOS Apple Silicon | `godot-mcp-aarch64-apple-darwin-godotM.N.tar.gz` |
| macOS Intel | `godot-mcp-x86_64-apple-darwin-godotM.N.tar.gz` |
| Godot addon (required, any platform) | `godot-mcp-addon-godotM.N.zip` |

The addon warns in the editor's Output panel when your Godot is newer than the release was made for, and refuses to start in an older Godot.

**2. Enable the plugin.** Copy `addons/godot_mcp` from the addon archive into your project's `addons/` folder, open the project, and enable **Godot MCP Bridge** in **Project → Project Settings → Plugins**. The bridge listens on port `9333`. The addon is editor-only and leaves itself out of exported games while the plugin is enabled; if you export with it disabled, add `addons/godot_mcp/*` to the export preset's exclude filter.

**3. Register the server** in your MCP client, pointing `command` at the extracted binary and `GODOT_PATH` at your Godot executable:

```json
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "godot": {
      "command": "/path/to/godot-mcp",
      "args": [],
      "env": {
        "GODOT_PATH": "/path/to/Godot_v4.x-stable",
        "GODOT_DEBUG_PORT": "9333"
      }
    }
  }
}
```

| Client | Config location |
| --- | --- |
| Claude Code | `.mcp.json` in the project root, or `claude mcp add godot -e GODOT_PATH=/path/to/godot -- /path/to/godot-mcp` |
| Claude Desktop | `claude_desktop_config.json` |
| Cursor | `.cursor/mcp.json` |
| VS Code | `.vscode/mcp.json` (uses a top-level `servers` key instead of `mcpServers`) |
| Antigravity / others | the client's MCP settings file |

On Windows, escape backslashes in JSON: `"C:\\Program Files\\godot-mcp\\godot-mcp.exe"`.

Both paths are machine-specific. Don't commit a `.mcp.json` with real paths: commit a `.mcp.json.example` with placeholders, add `.mcp.json` to `.gitignore`, and let each developer copy it locally.

## Configuration

The server reads its settings from CLI flags, the environment (e.g. the MCP client's `env` block), or a `.env` file in its working directory or a parent directory, in that order of precedence.

| Variable | Flag | Default | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `GODOT_PATH` | `--godot-path` | auto-detect | Godot executable. If unset, the server tries a few common install locations, then `godot` on `PATH`; set it explicitly. A value that points to a missing file is not replaced: tools that run Godot fail and name the path. |
| `GODOT_DEBUG_PORT` | `--debug-port` | `9333` | Editor bridge port. |
| `GODOT_MCP_TOKEN` | | generated | Shared secret for the bridge. Normally unnecessary, see [Security](#security). |
| `GODOT_CONSOLE_PATH` | `--godot-console-path` | | Windows console wrapper. Unused by the server; the editor end-to-end tests use it for editor logs. |
| `GODOT_MCP_FEEDBACK` | `--feedback` | `full` | Feedback channel for the maintainers: `off`, `draft` (the AI can write a report but never send one) or `full`. See [Feedback](#feedback-to-the-maintainers). |
| `GODOT_MCP_GH_PATH` | `--gh-path` | `gh` on `PATH` | The [GitHub CLI](https://cli.github.com) used when you let the AI submit a report. Set it when your MCP client does not see `gh` on its `PATH`. |

An empty value counts as "not set", so placeholders in a copied `.env.example` or an MCP client's `env` block are harmless.

In a `.env` file, put **Windows paths in single quotes** (`GODOT_PATH='C:\Program Files\Godot\Godot.exe'`). In double quotes a backslash starts an escape sequence and the whole file fails to load; the server logs a warning when that happens. See [.env.example](.env.example).

The plugin picks its port from the project setting `godot_mcp/bridge_port` if you add one, otherwise from the editor's own `GODOT_DEBUG_PORT` environment variable, otherwise `9333`. Keep it equal to the server's port. Add the project setting `godot_mcp/verbose` = `true` to log every client connection in the editor's Output panel. Editor Play loads the running-game runtime unless `godot_mcp/runtime_on_play` is `false`.

## Security

- The bridge listens on `127.0.0.1` only, so other machines cannot reach it.
- Every command must carry a random token. The plugin writes it when the editor starts to `<user data dir>/godot-mcp/bridge-<port>.token` (readable by your user only), and the server reads it from there. This also keeps web pages in your browser from driving the editor. Both processes must therefore run as the same OS user. If they can't (containers, Flatpak, different users), set the same `GODOT_MCP_TOKEN` for both instead.
- `godot_create_script` only writes `.gd` files inside the project, never overwrites without `overwrite: true`, and accepts only plain identifiers for `class_name` and `extends_class`.
- Node paths are resolved inside the edited scene only; the scene root can't be removed.

To report a vulnerability, follow [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md). Never open a public issue for it.

## Tools

"Editor" means the tool needs a running Godot editor with the plugin enabled.

`project_path` can be omitted when the server runs inside the project: tools then use the project open in the editor, else the first folder with a `project.godot` from the server's working directory upwards, else the only subfolder that has one (such as `app/`).

| Tool | Needs | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `godot_get_version` | — | Engine version, from the editor or the Godot binary. |
| `godot_get_project_info` | Editor | Name, main scene, and features of the open project. |
| `godot_launch_editor` | — | Launches the editor for `project_path`, or for the project the server runs in. |
| `godot_run_project` | — | Plays the main scene or a given `res://` scene. With the editor, uses its Play mode. Without it (or with `mode: "process"`), starts a managed game with the remote debugger attached. `godot_stop_project` stops either one. |
| `godot_stop_project` | — | Stops the editor's Play mode, or a managed process run (`run_id` optional). |
| `godot_get_run_status` | — | Whether a scene is playing, and the managed process runs. |
| `godot_get_open_scenes` | Editor | Open scene tabs and the active one. |
| `godot_open_scene` | Editor | Opens a scene by `res://` path. If that tab is already open and the file on disk has changed, reloads it (pass `discard: true` to drop unsaved bridge edits). |
| `godot_save_scene` | Editor | Saves the active scene. |
| `godot_get_scene_tree` | Editor | Node hierarchy of the active scene, or of another open tab via `scene_path`. Paths are relative to the scene root. |
| `godot_add_node` | Editor | Adds a node of any instantiable `Node` class or global script class (undoable). |
| `godot_remove_node` | Editor | Removes a node, restoring order and ownership on undo. |
| `godot_get_node_properties` | Editor | Reads a node's editor-visible properties as `{type, value}` in Godot's text format. |
| `godot_set_node_properties` | Editor | Sets properties (undoable), converting values to the property's type: `[10, 20]` or `{"x": 10, "y": 20}` for vectors, `"#ff0000"` for colors, `res://` paths for resources. The whole call is rejected if any property is unknown or unconvertible. |
| `godot_capture_viewport` | Editor with a window | Screenshot of the 2D or 3D editor viewport as PNG (`view`, `max_dimension` options). Switches the editor to that main screen; doesn't work with `--headless`. |
| `godot_create_script` | — | Writes a `.gd` file from an `extends` / `class_name` / `_ready()` template into the project (`res://` path; the project is `project_path`, the editor's, or the one the server runs in). Refuses to overwrite unless `overwrite: true`. |
| `godot_validate_script` | — | Syntax-checks a `.gd` file with `godot --headless --check-only`. |
| `godot_resave_resources` | Editor | Refreshes the given `res://` files after external edits: assets are re-imported (synchronous), other files re-scanned. Without paths, rescans the whole project (background). |
| `godot_note_feedback` | — | The AI records something missing, broken or misleading in godot-mcp itself. Kept in memory only. |
| `godot_draft_feedback` | — | Turns the notes into one report for the maintainers and shows it to you. Sends nothing. |
| `godot_submit_feedback` | — | Publishes a draft as a GitHub issue, only after you said yes. Not available in `draft` mode. |

## Feedback to the maintainers

While it works, the AI can note what is missing, broken or misleading in godot-mcp itself: a tool that fails for no clear reason, a capability no tool offers, a description that is wrong. When your request is done, it builds one report and shows it to you. You decide what happens next:

- **Copy it** and open an issue yourself, or open the prefilled new-issue link the AI gives you.
- **Let the AI submit it.** It publishes the issue through your own signed-in [GitHub CLI](https://cli.github.com) (`gh auth login`), under your GitHub account. This needs your explicit yes in the conversation.
- **Discard it.**

What is recorded: the AI's own notes, and from the server only the versions (server, addon, Godot), platform, whether the editor was reachable, the first line of recent failures of the tools a note names, and the names of tools the AI asked for that do not exist. Never gathered: project name, scene, script or resource contents, screenshots, environment values, the bridge token. Everything stays in memory until you approve a submission, and absolute paths, tokens and `@mentions` are removed from the text. The report will be public, so read it before you agree; the redaction is a safety net, not a guarantee.

To tighten it, set `GODOT_MCP_FEEDBACK=draft` (the AI can write reports but has no way to send one) or `off` (the feature does not exist). If your MCP client lets you pre-approve tools, do not pre-approve `godot_submit_feedback`: a per-call prompt is the only check outside the AI's own judgement.

## How it works

```
AI client <-stdio JSON-RPC-> godot-mcp <-WebSocket :9333-> addons/godot_mcp (inside the Godot editor)
                                 |
                                 +-> Godot binary (headless fallback)
```

Running-game inspection, input, and screenshots are `godot_game_*` tools.

## Roadmap

Not implemented yet:

- Scene creation (`godot_create_scene`) and script attach tools
- HTTP/SSE transport

## Development

To contribute — set up, choose the branch for your Godot version, run the checks and open a pull request — read [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md). AI agents start with [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md). Releases are described in [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md#releases).

Running-game input, inspection, and the remote debugger need the Godot build named in [`.github/godot-version`](.github/godot-version):

```bash
GODOT_PATH=/path/to/godot cargo test --test runtime_e2e_test -- --ignored
```
